Group revenue 11,981 12,187 13,223 13,554 13,147 13,410
Group EBITDA 2,899 2,778 3,240 3,253 2,958 3,084

Tubes drivers. Shipments are about 3.87mt in FY2026 (Q2 2026 946kt; Q4 guided above 1mt). They then grow to 4.03mt (+4%), 4.15mt (+3%), 4.11mt (-1%) and 4.19mt (+2%). The average selling price is about $2,990/t in FY2026, then $3,110 (+4%), $3,090 (-0.5%), $3,030 (-2%) and $3,030 (flat).

By region. North America stays at about 47-50% of revenue ($3,050m of $6,067m in the first half of 2026). South America is about 18-20%: Vaca Muerta OCTG grows, while pipeline line pipe is lost to imports. The Middle East, Africa, Europe and Asia make up the rest. The Middle East is depressed in 2026 by the Hormuz deferrals and recovers in the second half of 2027.

Consensus segment figures. Tubes $11,296m, $11,787m and $11,796m; Other $618m, $719m and $733m. Tubes EBITDA $2,538m, $2,813m and $2,682m.

The operating drivers behind the inputs

  1. US price (30-43% of sales reprice off PipeLogix, with a lag of one to two quarters). The seamless index rose from a trough of about $2,110/t (August 2024) to about $2,574/t (mid-2026), and is up 16% year to date (September 2026). Realised prices sit about 34% above the index. I assume about $2,850/t by end-2026, held through 2027. Realised US prices therefore rise about 8-10% in FY2027. They plateau in FY2028 as distributor restocking ends and imports and new US capacity respond, and fall about 5-6% in FY2029. This driver supplies about 60% of the FY2027 EBITDA increase.
  2. International prices. Formula contracts with national oil companies have a lag of two to three quarters and are held flat in nominal terms. Saudi and Argentine line-pipe share is under pressure.
  3. Volume. Offshore deepwater (about 20% of revenue) grows about 8% in 2027 and 6% in 2028; Saipem research has offshore capex up about 30% in 2027. Suriname and Guyana use Rig Direct. Vaca Muerta OCTG grows. US rigs are about 600 at end-2026 and flat in 2027. Artrom (200kt of seamless capacity) is consolidated from FY2027 at low margin.
  4. Strait of Hormuz. The base case has it closed through 2026, as guidance assumes, and reopening in mid-2027. About $130m a quarter of Gulf revenue returns from Q3 2027 with partial catch-up, at about 55-65% drop-through including about $30m a quarter of freight savings. This is the largest timing judgement in the inputs. If the Strait stays closed through 2027, FY2027 revenue is about $300m lower and EBITDA about $180m lower.
  5. Section 232 cost. The gross cost is $140m-150m a quarter. Net mitigation is +$80m in FY2027, +$150m in FY2028 and +$200m from FY2029 (Nucor Memphis bars, the Koppel upgrade in the second half of 2027, Sault Ste. Marie). Domestic bars carry a protected-market price, so only about a third of the gross cost is saved.
  6. Cost base. Fixed cash costs are $2.0bn-3.0bn, so the incremental margin on volume is about 45%. Fixed-cost inflation and North American regionalisation add about $60m-80m a year. Hot-rolled coil and scrap inflation reach cost of goods sold with a lag of about two quarters (inventory of about 140 days). I assume no new cost programme; in the FY2029 downturn there are about $50m a year of cuts.
  7. Working capital. It is 39.9% of trailing revenue at June 2026 (143 days). It ends FY2026 at about 40% and falls to 38.5% in FY2027 as Gulf stock ships. A return to 128-130 days, about $0.4bn-0.5bn of extra cash, is not in the base.
  8. Capex. First half of 2026 $236m. FY2027-28 is higher than historical levels because of the $90m Pennsylvania programme, the CAD 306m Sault Ste. Marie modernisation (net of CAD 148m of federal and provincial money) and Artrom.

The company's guidance, and how your inputs compare with it

  • Second half of 2026. Guided "in line with the first half" for sales and EBITDA (about $6.07bn and $1.38bn on the company basis), assuming Hormuz stays closed through year end. The third quarter is guided seasonally lower (European maintenance, mix) and the fourth quarter to volume above 1mt on higher prices. My second half is $6.12bn of revenue and $1.39bn of EBITDA, about 1% above guidance. That is consistent with the company's habit of guiding conservatively (EPS beats in 18 of 21 quarters). My third quarter is $2.92bn and $642m (Jefferies expects EBITDA broadly in line with the second quarter's $649m). My fourth quarter is $3.20bn and $752m.
  • FY2026 against guidance and consensus. Mine is $12.19bn and $2.78bn. Guidance implies about $12.1bn and $2.77bn. The FactSet median is $11.89bn and $2.73bn (brokers' basis); named brokers sit at $12.06bn-12.41bn and $2.75bn-2.83bn.
  • Mid-term. The EBITDA margin corridor is 20-25% through the cycle (2024 capital markets day). All my years sit inside it (22.5-24.5%). FY2027 at 24.5% is in the upper half, and needs price drop-through plus a partial Gulf return. Bull cases (Redburn 25.6%) sit above the corridor.
  • Capex. Guided "close to or slightly below historical levels" for 2026; mine is $540m. FY2027-28 at $640m-650m sits above "historical" because of the announced North American projects.
  • Capital return. There is no stated policy since the buyback was terminated in March 2026. The interim dividend was doubled to $0.59. My base assumes rising dividends and no buybacks.

The bridge from the company's own reported figures to the basis consensus uses

  • EBITDA. Company reported FY2025 was $2,899m. FactSet operating (the model's history line) was $2,911m (+0.4%). FactSet's brokers' basis actual was $2,881m (-0.6%). For the first half of 2026: company $1,384m, FactSet operating $1,408m (+1.7%). My margins are company basis. Subtract about 0.15 margin point to compare with consensus margins (brokers' basis FY2025 24.0% against company 24.2%). So my FY2027 EBITDA of $3,240m is about $3,220m on the brokers' basis, against a median of $3,053m and a mean of $3,158m.
  • EPS. Reported basic EPS equals the brokers' basis in FY2025 ($1.83). FactSet's adjusted diluted EPS was $1.86 and the Viewforth definition $1.93; neither is comparable with consensus. Consensus EPS implies about 1,011m, 1,027m and 1,036m shares for FY2026-28 against 1,009.6m in issue. On the true count, consensus is about $1.86, $2.10 and $2.17.
  • Net cash. The company's figure ($3,322m at end-2025; $3,564m at June 2026) includes about $1.2bn of non-current investments. FactSet's net cash (cash and short-term investments less debt) was $2,430m and $2,336m. FactSet's market-cap snapshot uses 1,072m diluted shares, which overstates market cap and enterprise value by about 6%.
  • Free cash flow. Mine is operating cash flow less capex including intangibles, before leases, acquisitions and dividends, the same as FactSet's consensus definition. FactSet's historical "free cash flow" line deducts dividends and is not comparable.
  • Dividend per share. FactSet records it by payment period. The FY2026 consensus of $1.09 looks understated against $0.60 paid in May and $0.59 payable in November 2026.

Your cross-check: your own earnings per share and free cash flow for each of the five fiscal years

Cross-check FY2026 (year to 31-Dec-26) FY2027 (year to 31-Dec-27) FY2028 (year to 31-Dec-28) FY2029 (year to 31-Dec-29) FY2030 (year to 31-Dec-30)
Earnings per share 1.94 2.15 2.17 1.95 2.05
Free cash flow 1,770 1,900 2,100 2,110 1,960

How the cross-check is built. Shares are about 1,010m in FY2026 and 1,009.6m thereafter. EBITDA less depreciation gives EBIT of 2,168, 2,610, 2,613, 2,313 and 2,434. Adding net financial items including associates gives pre-tax profit of 2,458, 2,830, 2,843, 2,553 and 2,684. Less tax at the rates above and less minorities, net income to shareholders is 1,958, 2,171, 2,195, 1,970 and 2,074.

Free cash flow is operating cash flow less capex including intangibles, before lease payments, acquisitions and dividends. Operating cash flow is about 2,310, 2,550, 2,740, 2,730 and 2,580. It is built from EBITDA less cash tax (about 450, 590, 580, 525 and 550), less the working-capital change at the percentages above, plus interest and associate dividends, less about 60 of other items. After lease payments, free cash flow is about 1,715, 1,845, 2,045, 2,050 and 1,900.

Company-basis net cash at year end is about 3,640, 4,050, 4,510, 4,970 and 5,230.

Research requests for the next set

Company: Tenaris S.A. (TEN-IT; NYSE ADS: TS), the global leader in seamless and welded steel pipe and related services for the oil and gas industry; segments are Tubes (OCTG casing and tubing, premium connections, line pipe and coated line pipe, sold largely through its Rig Direct supply-and-service model; about 95% of sales per company disclosure) and Other (sucker rods, coiled tubing, Argentine oilfield services and industrial pipe).

Request: T-D1

Peers: Vallourec (VK-FR: closest premium seamless peer, now at margin parity after its German closures and Brazil programme, 100% locally made US steel, sells through distributors); Nippon Steel and its subsidiary U.S. Steel (integrated US tubular competitor investing in Fairfield after the 2025 merger); TMK (Russian number two by volume, shut out of Western markets); JFE Steel (Japanese premium seamless producer and Tenaris's 13Cr alloy supplier); Hunting PLC (HTG-GB: asset-light premium threader exposed to the same US cycle); Schoeller-Bleckmann (SBO-AT: US land-activity sensitivity); Welspun Corp (Indian welded and line pipe maker expanding US capacity and winning Argentine tenders); the Saudi national champion formed from JESCO and AMTJ under the Public Investment Fund (local-content rival for Aramco); Ternium (sister company and associate, also exposed to Section 232). Operating conditions: a US onshore upcycle under 50% Section 232 tariffs with distributor restocking (2025-27); a US onshore downturn with a falling rig count (2015-16, 2019-20, 2024); relaxation or removal of US steel tariffs leading to an import surge; an oil price fall with WTI below $65; the Strait of Hormuz closed (since March 2026) and then reopened; a deepwater offshore upcycle (Brazil, Guyana, Suriname, the Black Sea); steel input inflation (hot-rolled coil, scrap, tariffed billets and bars); liberalising markets with open tenders (Argentina). Inputs: How plausible is a recovery in Tenaris's EBITDA margin from the second quarter of 2026 (21.9%, company disclosure of 5 August 2026) towards the upper end of its 20-25% through-cycle corridor in 2027, as some brokers publish (Redburn modelled 24.5% for 2027 on 22 May 2026), given that such a recovery would rest on US seamless price increases reaching realised prices with a lag, a return of deferred Middle East shipments, and offshore volume growth? Which of the strengths the plan relies on has Tenaris demonstrably shown in past cycles: its realised price premium to the PipeLogix index (about 34%, per Bernstein, July 2025), Rig Direct customer retention, margin resilience in downturns (2016, 2020, 2024), and the delivery of the 2020 fixed-cost cut ($230m a year) and the 2024-25 $200m cost programme? Which strengths would it need to acquire: self-sufficiency in US steel bars to replace tariffed imports (the Nucor Steel Memphis framework from January 2026; the $90m Koppel and Ambridge programme announced 24 June 2026; the CAD 306m Sault Ste. Marie modernisation announced 22 May 2026); working-capital discipline (143 days at June 2026 against 128 a year earlier); and commercial traction in geothermal and carbon capture? Has any OCTG producer previously replaced imported semi-finished steel with domestic supply under tariffs (for example after the 2018 Section 232 introduction), and how long did it take and what did it cost? How did Tenaris's and Vallourec's margins behave in earlier price recoveries after a trough (2017-18, 2021-22), and how much of each recovery came from price, volume and cost? How did Tenaris recover Middle East revenue after earlier logistics or geopolitical disruptions, and did any deferred volume become lost volume? Focus: Whether Tenaris's expected 2027 margin recovery rests on strengths it has demonstrated through past cycles (its index premium, Rig Direct, contract stability, cost flexibility) or on strengths it has yet to prove (domestic US steel supply replacing tariffed imports, working-capital normalisation), and what the precedents say about the speed and completeness of such recoveries.

Layer 3 and Layer 4: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months / Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

Written 26 October 2026, 11:31 to 11:49 UK, Fable, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's title
Tenaris (TEN-IT): answers to the third set (predictability of the forecasts) and the fourth set (guidance, consensus and narrative)

Written 2026-10-26 for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo. Money is in US dollars, millions unless stated; Tenaris reports in dollars. "Company basis" means Tenaris's own reported figures; "brokers' basis" means FactSet consensus. The first set's answers are cited as C-xx, the second set's as P-xx. The AlphaSense reports are cited by template (T-D1, T-D2, T-I1, T-I2) or as OWN-1 to OWN-6. "The base case" means the second set's forecast inputs as the model calculated them (file 03), unless I say "my revised base", which means those inputs with the changes recorded in inputs-changed.md.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes

Reading notes: what is new since the second set wrote, and what the model's cross-check gaps mean

  1. The US price leg moved 9% in one month. Jefferies (26 October 2026): the US seamless OCTG index rose 9% month on month in October to above $3,100/t, the largest monthly dollar gain since July 2008; welded rose 10% to about $2,600/t; year to date the gains are 26% (seamless) and 33% (welded). US OCTG imports fell 27% month on month in August to 110kt (seamless +48% to about 42kt, welded -44% to 69kt). Distributor inventories are now below their long-term average (Morgan Stanley, 15 July 2026; Jefferies, 26 October 2026), having been about seven months in October 2025 and just over six months in March 2026 against a 5.5-month median. The second set's inputs assumed an index of about $2,850/t at end-2026, held through 2027. The index is already 9% above that. This is the largest change in the evidence and it is why I change the FY2027 inputs.
  2. The cost side moved too. US hot-rolled coil is at $1,375-1,385/t (Deutsche Bank, October 2026), up $380/t since January. Management put the welded (ERW) margin squeeze at about $350/t, or $35-40m a quarter (Q4 2025 call). The Nucor Hickman coil contract expires in December 2026. Nucor raised spot prices more than 20 times in 2026 (Jefferies, September 2026), so domestic bars replace tariffed imports at protected prices. Part of October's price gain is therefore cost pass-through, not margin.
  3. Tariff mitigation is further along than the second set assumed, but it is already in the base. On 7 May 2026 management said the quarterly tariff cost had fallen to $110-120m from about $150m. That saving sits inside the first-half 2026 margin of 22.8% from which the inputs start, so it does not change the path. It does mean the second set's "about $570m net cost in FY2026" overstates the current run-rate by roughly $100m a year, and that the remaining mitigation (Koppel's $90m upgrade into the second half of 2027; Sault Ste. Marie over 2027-28) is modest, as P-03 assumed.
  4. The dividend signal was misread in the first set, and the second set carried the misreading. Reading note 6 of the first set dismissed Redburn's "6.8% yield" as not fitting the figures. It fits once the interim-to-final pattern is known: the 2025 interim was $0.29 and the final $0.60 (paid May 2026), so the interim was one-third of the year's total. Jefferies (6 August 2026) reads the doubled $0.59 interim as "one-third of the annual payment", implying about $1.80 a share and about $1.8bn a year; Redburn's 6.8% is $1.80 on the August share price; Jefferies' "annualised run-rate of at least $1.8bn" says the same. On the Q2 call the CEO said the board chose dividends over buybacks for "simplicity and liquidity", and Ternium's CEO said in February 2026 that Ternium does not buy back shares because its float is limited. The reading that fits all of this is that the ordinary dividend is replacing the buyback at roughly the old total distribution level, not that total distributions have been halved. I raise the dividend inputs from FY2027 (see inputs-changed.md). The final dividend proposed with the FY2026 results on 18 February 2027 settles it.
  5. Hormuz: the published reopening scenarios have moved later, and the oil link is weaker than the first set feared. The IMF's October baseline assumes a gradual reopening from March 2027 with full normalisation by October 2027 (via NBG, 20 October 2026). Deutsche Bank (20 October 2026) models a settlement in early 2027 taking Brent to $74. Middle East crude exports are already at 94% of pre-war volumes through pipeline bypasses and escorted shuttles (Morgan Stanley, 21 October 2026), so Brent at $105-111 carries a risk premium rather than a physical shortage, and a reopening could cut it sharply. Tenaris's deferrals run at 30-40kt a quarter, $120-130m (Jefferies, 15 September 2026); one Tenaris vessel sent in March was stuck until late August; Vallourec trucks two-thirds of its Gulf deliveries through Oman and reports no cancellations; Tenaris's Middle East EBITDA exposure is about 12% (Bernstein, 19 March 2026). The second set's mid-2027 reopening sits inside the IMF path and I keep it.
  6. Working-capital days are cyclical, not a new high. Operating working capital days were 134 at end-2023, 145 at end-2024, 136 at March 2026 and 143 at June 2026 (Tenaris releases). The first set's "deteriorating standards" (C-14) overstates the slip; the second set's 38-40% of revenue is right.
  7. The dated events of the next six months, in order: 3 November US midterms; 4 November Q3 results (consensus EBITDA $656m per Jefferies; the second set has $642m); Vallourec's Q3 before Tenaris (Bernstein, 12 October: "3Q26 may be the last stop in Hormuz"); December 2026 the Department of Commerce final scope ruling on mechanical pipe (Customs and Border Protection's $49.6m claim) and the final Mexican anti-dumping second-review rate (preliminary 1.62%); December 2026 Nucor Hickman expiry; Q4 Artrom closing (EU approval 16 October 2026); December to February operators' 2027 budgets; 18 February 2027 FY2026 results with the first-half 2027 outlook and the final dividend; 12 May 2027 AGM.

The model's cross-check gaps, explained. The one-page summary shows the model's EPS above the second set's cross-check by $0.06-0.07 in FY2027-30 (2.21 against 2.15; 2.24 against 2.17; 2.01 against 1.95; 2.11 against 2.05) and $0.01 below it in FY2026, and the model's free cash flow above the cross-check by about $45m in every year. Both gaps have one cause and neither shows an input to be wrong. The summary states "Not modelled: minority interests and hybrid bond coupons (Richard, 16-Oct-26)". The model's net income before minority interests of 2,234.9 in FY2027 equals the second set's 2,171 to shareholders plus the 65 of minorities the second set deducted; over 1,009.6m shares that is 2.21 against 2.15. The same arithmetic holds for FY2028-30 (minorities of 65, 60 and 60, about $0.06 a share). The FY2026 difference of $0.01 is immaterial and arises from how the model carries the first-half actual tax charge against the 17.5% full-year rate. The free cash flow gap of about $45m a year has the same sign every year and is consistent with the model carrying no cash leakage to minority holders (Tenaris's minorities earned $38.5m in the first half of 2026); at about 2% I leave it. Conclusion: no forecast input is wrong on account of the cross-check. The Consumer must read the model's EPS as before minorities and subtract about $0.06 a share before comparing it with reported EPS or with consensus, both of which are after minorities. The inputs I do change (below) are changed for other reasons.

Where I differ from the first and second sets, in one place

  1. Dividend per share paid. Second set: $1.19, $1.40, $1.55, $1.55, $1.60. Mine: $1.19, $1.70, $1.80, $1.80, $1.80 (reading note 4). This is the largest change to the model's output: it removes most of the cash accumulation (net cash ends 2030 at about $4.0bn rather than $5.2bn on the company basis) and raises the dividend component of total shareholder return.
  2. FY2027 revenue growth and margin. Second set: +8.5% and 24.5%. Mine: +10.0% and 24.9%, from the October index net of higher input costs (reading notes 1 and 2). FY2028 and FY2029 become +0.5% at 24.0% and -4.0% at 22.0% (from +2.5% at 24.0% and -3.0% at 22.5%), because a higher tariff-and-restocking peak normalises harder. FY2026 revenue growth moves from +1.7% to +1.9% as part of the October rise reaches fourth-quarter invoicing. My EPS path after minorities is therefore $1.95, $2.23, $2.16, $1.85, $1.99 against the second set's $1.94, $2.15, $2.17, $1.95, $2.05.
  3. Net financial items FY2028-30 trimmed to 220, 215, 215 (from 230, 240, 250) because cash no longer accumulates when the dividend absorbs free cash flow.
  4. First set C-14 and C-19. Working capital is cyclical, not deteriorating (reading note 6); the "restart or special likely in 2027" is right in substance but the form is a doubled ordinary dividend, not a buyback or a special.
  5. Second set P-10 scenario weights for FY2027. I move the bull case from 25% to 30% and the bear from 20% to 15% on the October price and inventory data (D-04).

The third set: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months

The Investment Analyst's group headings, as written Group: Risks · Group: Guidance · Group: Sell-side estimates · Group: Stock market narrative

The Investment Analyst's heading (Layer 4) The fourth set: Assessing the extent to which our assessment is reflected in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative

Forecast inputs changed by the third and fourth sets: Tenaris S.A. (TEN-IT), as at 26-Oct-26

Money in millions of USD, except per-share figures; costs and outflows negative. Only the lines changed are shown; every other line in Parts 1 to 3 of the second set's forecast inputs stands. Part 2 (cash flow and balance sheet inputs) is unchanged.

Part 1. Profit and loss inputs, one column for each of the five fiscal years

Revenue growth (%) 1.9 10.0 0.5 -4.0 2.0

The US seamless OCTG index rose 9% in October 2026 to above $3,100/t (+26% year to date; Jefferies, 26 October 2026) against the $2,850/t at end-2026 the second set assumed, so with 30-43% of sales repricing off the index after a one-to-two-quarter lag FY2027 revenue is about 1.5 points higher, a little of it reaching the fourth quarter of 2026; a higher tariff-and-restocking peak normalises harder as imports (seamless +48% month on month in August) and dated US capacity respond, so FY2028 is flatter and FY2029 falls further.

Input FY2026 (year to 31-Dec-26) FY2027 (year to 31-Dec-27) FY2028 (year to 31-Dec-28) FY2029 (year to 31-Dec-29) FY2030 (year to 31-Dec-30)
EBITDA margin (%) 22.8 24.9 24.0 22.0 23.0

The higher 2027 index adds about $150-250m of EBITDA at 55-65% first-year drop-through, offset by about $50-80m of extra input cost (hot-rolled coil at $1,375-1,385/t, the Nucor Hickman renewal in December 2026, a $35-40m a quarter welded squeeze), lifting FY2027 by 0.4 point; FY2028 holds 24.0% because volume and the Gulf offset the start of the price fade; FY2029 is 0.5 point lower because a price-led fall from a higher peak carries a decremental margin of about 70%.

Net financial items 290 220 220 215 215

With the dividend absorbing most free cash flow (next line) net cash stays near $3.6-4.0bn rather than rising to $5.2bn, so interest income does not grow with the balance and falls with dollar rates; associates stay at about $95m a year.

Input FY2026 (year to 31-Dec-26) FY2027 (year to 31-Dec-27) FY2028 (year to 31-Dec-28) FY2029 (year to 31-Dec-29) FY2030 (year to 31-Dec-30)
Dividend per share 1.19 1.70 1.80 1.80 1.80

The 2025 interim ($0.29) was one-third of the year's total ($0.89), the 2026 interim was doubled to $0.59 with the CEO citing "simplicity and liquidity" as the reason for choosing dividends over buybacks (Q2 2026 call), Jefferies (6 August 2026) reads the interim as one-third of the annual payment and Redburn's 6.8% yield (20 August 2026) implies about $1.80 a share, so I assume a final of about $1.10 paid in May 2027 and an interim of about $0.60 in November 2027 ($1.70 paid in 2027), then $1.80 a year (about $1.82bn, roughly 90-95% of free cash flow after leases), which replaces the 2024-25 buybacks rather than halving total distributions; the probability I attach to a final at or above $1.00 is about 60%, and the February 2027 announcement settles it.

The October index rise reaches invoicing with a one-to-two-quarter lag, so the fourth quarter of 2026 gains a little (revenue about $3,220m on volume above 1mt) and the first three quarters of 2027 gain most (about $3,340-3,370m each, including the Gulf return from the third quarter); the fourth quarter of 2027 slows as realised US prices begin to fade and the comparison base is higher.

Price drop-through lifts each 2027 quarter by about 0.5 point over the second set's path (peaking at the top of the 20-25% corridor in the third quarter, when the Gulf catch-up and freight savings also land), while the fourth quarter of 2027 turns down as the price fade meets a fixed-cost base that has risen with the upturn; the third quarter of 2026 is unchanged because it is already in the books.

Appendix The share-price co-movement table the Investment Analyst was given

Co-movement of TEN-IT with its dashboard peers (computed 2026-10-23 from the master dashboard's daily prices)

What this is: daily close-to-close returns over rolling windows of 126 trading days (about six months). Correlation: how closely the daily moves line up (1 = always together, 0 = unrelated). Beta: how far the stock moves for a 1% move in the basket. R-squared: the share of the stock's daily price variance the peer basket explains; the remainder is the stock's own (idiosyncratic) movement. Basket: the peers below, equal-weighted. Prices only; the multiple's own history is in the financials block.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Tenaris S.A. (TEN-IT; NYSE ADS: TS), the global leader in seamless and welded steel pipe and related services for the oil and gas industry; segments are Tubes (OCTG casing and tubing, premium connections, line pipe and coated line pipe, sold largely through its Rig Direct supply-and-service model; about 95% of sales per company disclosure) and Other (sucker rods, coiled tubing, Argentine oilfield services and industrial pipe).

Request: OWN-1

Question: For Tenaris S.A. (TEN-IT), what do the company's own dividend history and statements, Techint group precedents and the Milan listing rules imply for the final dividend for 2026 that the board will propose with the full-year results on 18 February 2027? Cover: the interim and final dividend per share declared for each year since 2010 and the ratio of interim to total in each year; what management said about dividend policy and share liquidity on the 6 August 2026 call after doubling the interim dividend to $0.59 per share (from $0.29 the year before) and after terminating the second tranche of its $1.2 billion buyback on 3 March 2026; how Ternium S.A. and other Techint group companies have set dividends when their free float was constrained; what the Borsa Italiana free-float requirement is and how it has been applied to other controlled companies; how named brokers (Jefferies on 6 August 2026 described the interim as one-third of the annual payment; Rothschild & Co Redburn on 20 August 2026 cited a 6.8% yield) have modelled the 2026 total dividend and 2027-28 distributions; and what the sister companies' capital expenditure plans (Ternium's Pesquería programme; Tecpetrol's Vaca Muerta projects under the RIGI regime) imply for the controlling shareholder's cash preferences. State the evidence for and against each possible outcome and which filings or statements before February 2027 would resolve it.

Request: OWN-2

Question: Jefferies reported on 26 October 2026 that the US seamless OCTG index rose 9% month on month in October 2026 to above $3,100 per metric ton, the largest monthly dollar gain since July 2008, and that distributor inventories were below their long-term average while US OCTG imports fell 27% month on month in August 2026. For Tenaris S.A. (TEN-IT) and its US competitors (Vallourec, U.S. Steel within Nippon Steel, Welspun Corp, Borusan Pipe, SeAH Steel), what happened after each previous episode of a record or near-record monthly rise in US OCTG prices (2008, 2011-12, the 2018 Section 232 introduction, 2021-22): how many further months did prices rise, when and how far did they fall over the following 12 to 24 months, what happened to monthly import volumes by origin and to distributor months of supply, how long after the price peak did Tenaris's realised tube price per tonne and EBITDA margin peak and then trough, and how did sell-side estimates and ratings for Tenaris behave through each turn? Cover also the dated US capacity additions (Welspun's Little Rock mills; U.S. Steel's Fairfield quench-and-temper line and premium thread line; Tenaris's Koppel programme), the pending anti-dumping cases against Austria, Taiwan and the UAE, and transhipment enforcement, and say which indicators have historically signalled the price peak earliest and by how many months.

Request: OWN-3

Question: For each broker publishing on Tenaris S.A. (TEN-IT) (Jefferies, Morgan Stanley, Bernstein, Rothschild & Co Redburn, Goldman Sachs, Barclays, Intermonte, Equita, Banca Akros, Oddo BHF, Intesa Sanpaolo, TD Cowen, iM Securities and any other identifiable house), what does its latest published model assume for 2026, 2027 and 2028 on each of: the US seamless OCTG price per tonne and the lag to Tenaris's realised prices; tube shipments in tonnes and average selling price per tonne; the date the Strait of Hormuz reopens and the treatment of the deferred Middle East shipments; the quarterly Section 232 tariff cost and its mitigation; the EBITDA margin; net financial income and the tax rate; the dividend per share and any share buyback; and the number of shares used for earnings per share (Tenaris had 1,009,639,756 shares in issue after the cancellation of 62,355,174 treasury shares on 12 May 2026)? Date each model, say which were last updated before and after the 1 October and 26 October 2026 Jefferies pricing notes and before and after the 5 August 2026 second-quarter results, and set out where the models cluster and where they differ most, so that it is clear which assumptions the FactSet median consensus rests on and which brokers would have to change which inputs for consensus to move after the 4 November 2026 and February 2027 results.
